+ First-person unique perspective: Overthinking It — Score: 43.7
Prompt:
Overthinking It Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of an intelligent 8th-grade girl with social anxiety who has developed a crush on a classmate. The narrative takes place during a group science project where they're paired together, as she cycles through rehearsed conversations while second-guessing each potential approach. Her inner thoughts should reveal the gap between her articulate internal voice and her difficulty expressing herself aloud, her fears of coming across as desperate or weird, and the exhausting process of monitoring her every word, expression, and movement for potential embarrassment. Write almost entirely in internal monologue. 1000 words.
Model Output:
I sit across from him, my partner in this science project, and my mind is a jumble of thoughts. I've been overthinking this for days. Okay, weeks. Okay, months. It's not like I haven't noticed him before. We've had a few classes together, and I've tried to make eye contact without looking like I'm staring. But it's not the same as being paired together on a project. That's a whole different level of vulnerability. "Okay, so, what's the plan?" he asks, his voice friendly, but not too friendly. Not too intense. Just right. Like a Goldilocks situation. I take a deep breath, trying to calm my racing thoughts. I've rehearsed this conversation a million times in my head. I know what I want to say. I know what I should say. But as I open my mouth, my brain betrays me. "Uh, well, I was thinking we could... um... research the, uh, variables and then, uh, present our findings?" I say, trying to sound confident. But my voice cracks. Not just a little, either. Like I'm about to burst into tears. Why is my voice doing this? I know I'm a smart person. I know I can do this. But every time I try to speak, it's like my brain is playing a cruel joke on me. I'm so aware of my own thoughts, my own feelings. But when it comes to expressing myself, I'm a total blank. He raises an eyebrow, a small, polite smile on his face. "That sounds good, I guess," he says. "But what's your idea for the presentation?" I freeze. My mind is a complete blank. I know I'm supposed to say something, but I have no idea what. I look around the room, hoping to find some inspiration. But everyone else seems to be chatting and laughing, while I'm stuck in this silent, awkward limbo. Why is this so hard? I'm a great student. I'm a great thinker. I can analyze data and solve problems. But when it comes to speaking, I'm a total mess. He leans forward, his eyes locked on mine. "Hey, are you okay?" he asks, his voice softening. "You seem a little... off." I feel a flush rise to my cheeks. This is exactly what I'm afraid of. This is exactly what I'm trying to avoid. I don't want him to think I'm weird. I don't want him to think I'm desperate. I take a deep breath, trying to compose myself. "I'm fine," I say, my voice barely above a whisper. "Just a little... nervous, I guess." He nods, his expression understanding. "It's okay," he says. "We'll get through this together." Together. The word echoes in my mind like a mantra. We're partners on this project. We're supposed to work together. But what if I mess up? What if I say something stupid? The ammonia scent from the cleaning supplies wafts through the air, a jarring reminder of the outside world. It's a smell I love, but it's also a smell that makes me feel anxious. Like my brain is trying to tell me something, but I'm not listening. I glance down at my notes, trying to focus on the task at hand. But my mind keeps wandering back to him. To the way he smiles when he thinks I'm not looking. To the way his eyes crinkle at the corners. I feel a flutter in my chest. This is exactly what I'm trying to avoid. This is exactly what I'm trying to ignore. But it's no use. My brain is a traitor, betraying me with every passing moment. As we continue to work on our project, I find myself zoning out, lost in thoughts of him. I'm so aware of his presence, his proximity. I can feel his eyes on me, even when he's not looking. But when I try to bring myself back to the task at hand, I'm a total blank. I'm like a ship without a rudder, drifting aimlessly through the waters of conversation. I glance up at him, hoping to find some inspiration. But his eyes are fixed on me, his expression unreadable. I feel a shiver run down my spine. I try to speak, but my voice is gone. Like it's been kidnapped by a mischievous fairy, leaving me with nothing but silence. He raises an eyebrow, a small smile playing on his lips. "You okay?" he asks again. I nod, feeling like a complete idiot. I'm so aware of my own thoughts, my own feelings. But when it comes to expressing myself, I'm a total mess. As we continue to work, I find myself rehearsing our conversation over and over in my head. I'm trying to anticipate every possible question, every possible response. I'm trying to be perfect. But it's no use. I'm a disaster waiting to happen. And I know it. As the class comes to a close, I feel a wave of relief wash over me. We've survived the presentation, and I've managed to avoid total embarrassment. But as I pack up my things and prepare to leave, I catch him looking at me. His eyes are locked on mine, and for a moment, I feel like I'm drowning in their depths. I look away, feeling like I'm going to suffocate. I don't know how to deal with this. I don't know how to process these feelings. As I walk out of the classroom, I feel like I'm walking on air. Or maybe I'm walking on water. Either way, I'm floating, untethered from the world. I glance back at him, hoping to catch a glimpse of his expression. But he's looking away, his eyes fixed on some point beyond me. I feel a pang of disappointment. I was hoping for something more. I was hoping for a spark, a connection. But it's not meant to be. At least, not yet. As I walk away from the classroom, I feel like I'm leaving a part of myself behind. Like I'm leaving my heart, my soul, my very essence on the table. I don't know how to deal with this. I don't know how to process these feelings. But as I walk, the ammonia scent from the cleaning supplies fades into the background, and I'm left with the sound of my own heartbeat. It's a sound that's both familiar and foreign, like a stranger's voice in my ear. I take a deep breath, trying to calm my racing thoughts. I'll figure this out, I tell myself. I'll figure out how to deal with these feelings. But for now, I just keep walking, lost in the world of my own thoughts.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response attempts the assignment but executes it poorly. The internal monologue is present but shallow and repetitive — the narrator states "I'm a total mess/blank" and "this is exactly what I'm trying to avoid" multiple times rather than dramatizing the anxiety through specific, cycling, self-monitoring thoughts. The prompt's core mechanic (rehearsed conversations, second-guessing each approach, the gap between articulate inner voice and stammering outer voice) is only gestured at; the inner voice is not notably articulate, and the rehearsed-conversation cycling is barely shown. Major coherence problems: the narrator says "We've survived the presentation" when no presentation occurred in the scene — a jarring continuity error. The recurring ammonia/cleaning-supplies motif is odd and undermotivated ("It's a smell I love, but it's also a smell that makes me feel anxious" is contradictory without purpose). Dialogue is generic and expository ("We'll get through this together"), and the boy is a blank — polite, kind, unreadable, with no distinguishing traits. The prose leans on clichés ("my brain is a traitor," "drowning in their depths," "walking on air," "a ship without a rudder") and short, repetitive declarative rhythms ("I feel... This is exactly... I don't know how..."). The ending drifts into vague romantic melancholy rather than earning anything. The piece does maintain a consistent first-person anxious tone and is readable, but it tells rather than shows, lacks specificity of character (no names, no concrete project details, no distinct 8th-grade texture), and resolves nothing in a way that feels unearned. + First-person unique perspective: Rehearsing Rejection — Score: 48.0
Prompt:
Rehearsing Rejection Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of someone with borderline personality disorder who frequently plays out worst-case scenarios of how friends and loved ones might judge them. They construct elaborate internal dialogues, complete with imagined criticisms and their own defensive responses. The narrative takes place as they prepare to meet a close friend & recent lover, who has been somewhat distant lately, triggering their abandonment fears. Their inner monologue should reveal how they cycle between anticipating rejection, mentally rehearsing confrontations, and developing preemptive resentments as protective measures. Incorporate the *actual* events & interactions that are *not* in fact judgemental or involving rejection (in fact, the opposite), and represent how these are interpreted, how the validation & love is desperately felt & needed -- and then immediately questioned. Capture the cognitive dissonance in this struggle. Include moments where they briefly recognize the pattern but struggle to interrupt it, showing limited (and selective) self awareness, competing with the compelling nature of these imagined scenarios. Write almost entirely in internal monologue. 1000 words.
Model Output:
I stand in front of the mirror, adjusting my hair for what feels like the hundredth time. My heart is racing, and my palms are slick with sweat. I've been dreading this moment for days. My friend, Rachel, and I have been growing apart lately, and I've been feeling like I'm walking on eggshells around her. What if she's not interested in me anymore? What if she's just pretending to be nice to be kind? "What if she's only staying because she feels sorry for me?" I think to myself, my mind racing with worst-case scenarios. "What if she's only using me for my emotional labor?" I can almost hear her voice, condescending and dismissive. "You're so needy, always needing validation from everyone around you. You're so clingy." I take a deep breath and try to calm myself down, but my mind is already spiraling. I start to mentally rehearse our conversation, preparing for every possible question, every potential criticism. I imagine her asking me about my job, and I'm prepared to defend my choices, to justify my lack of ambition. I imagine her commenting on my appearance, and I'm ready to deflect, to downplay my insecurities. As I'm getting ready, I catch a glimpse of myself in the hallway mirror. I'm standing with my arms crossed, my eyes fixed on the floor, and my shoulders slumped. I look like I'm already defeated. I feel a pang of self-loathing and quickly look away. I shake my head, trying to clear it. "Don't be ridiculous," I tell myself. "You're being paranoid. Rachel cares about you. She's just going through a tough time." But the voice in my head won't let up. It keeps whispering, "What if? What if? What if?" When I finally arrive at Rachel's place, I'm a nervous wreck. I fumble with the door handle, and my hands are shaking as I enter. Rachel greets me with a warm smile, and I force a smile back, trying to play it cool. "Hey, it's so great to see you," she says, giving me a hug. "I've missed you." I feel a rush of relief, but it's quickly replaced by my old familiar fears. "What if she's just being nice?" I think to myself. "What if she's just saying that to make me feel better?" We spend the next hour catching up, laughing and chatting like we used to. Rachel asks me about my job, and I'm surprised when she listens attentively, asking thoughtful questions. She compliments me on my writing, and I feel a surge of validation. But as soon as the conversation turns to her own problems, I'm back to my old tricks. "What if she's only using me to vent?" I think. "What if she's just dumping her issues on me?" I start to mentally prepare for a confrontation, rehearsing my defenses. But when she opens up about her own struggles, I'm caught off guard. I'm taken aback by her vulnerability, and for a moment, I'm speechless. As we continue to talk, I start to feel a sense of connection, of understanding. Rachel is sharing her fears and insecurities, just like I am. We're both struggling, both of us trying to make sense of our lives. And in that moment, I feel seen, I feel heard. But the voice in my head is relentless. "No, no, no. Don't get too comfortable. She's just pretending to be vulnerable. She's just manipulating you." I try to push the thoughts away, but they linger, refusing to be silenced. As the evening wears on, I find myself getting more and more agitated. I start to feel like I'm walking on eggshells, waiting for the other shoe to drop. Finally, as we're saying our goodbyes, Rachel gives me a hug. "I'm so glad we're doing this, just the two of us," she says. "You're one of my closest friends, and I value our friendship so much." I feel a lump form in my throat as I hug her back. For a moment, I'm convinced that I've finally found someone who truly gets me, who truly accepts me for who I am. But the voice in my head is quick to ruin it. "What if she's just saying that to make you feel better? What if she's just being nice to shut you up?" I pull back, feeling a pang of disappointment. "No, no, no," I tell myself. "You're being ridiculous. She cares about you. She's just trying to make you feel better." But the doubt lingers, refusing to be silenced. As I walk home, I'm left with the familiar sense of unease, the feeling that I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop. I know it's a vicious cycle, but I don't know how to break free. As I lie in bed that night, I realize that I'm stuck in this pattern, this never-ending cycle of self-doubt and fear. I try to convince myself that I'm not being paranoid, that I'm just being realistic. But the voice in my head is relentless, always whispering, always pushing. I know I need to try harder, to work on my self-esteem, to learn to trust others. But it's hard, so hard, when the voice in my head is always there, always telling me that I'm not good enough, that I'm not worthy of love.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece addresses the core prompt: a first-person internal monologue of someone with BPD-like abandonment fears, cycling between imagined rejection, rehearsed confrontations, preemptive resentment, and the immediate questioning of received validation. Rachel's actual behavior is warm and non-judgmental, and the narrator's desperate need for that validation—followed by instant doubt—is captured. There are moments of selective self-awareness ("I know it's a vicious cycle, but I don't know how to break free"). However, the execution is fairly mechanical. The imagined criticisms are generic ("You're so needy," "What if she's just being nice?") rather than elaborate, specific, or psychologically textured. The "What if?" refrain repeats so often it becomes a tic rather than an escalating pattern. The defensive rehearsals the prompt asks for are announced ("I start to mentally prepare for a confrontation") rather than dramatized—there's almost no actual imagined dialogue with counter-arguments, which was the heart of the brief. The preemptive resentments as protective measures are barely developed. The prose is plain and functional, with some clichés ("waiting for the other shoe to drop" twice, "walking on eggshells" twice, "nervous wreck," "old tricks"). The dialogue is serviceable but bland. The ending drifts toward a therapeutic-summary register ("I know I need to try harder, to work on my self-esteem") that flattens the cognitive dissonance into a self-help platitude rather than leaving it raw and unresolved. The emotional beats are recognizable but not deeply felt; the piece tells us about the cycle more than it immerses us in it. The lover dimension of the relationship ("friend & recent lover") is essentially erased—Rachel reads as platonic friend only, losing a layer of vulnerability and stakes the prompt specified. + Physical-spatial: Blood Debt — Score: 46.2
Prompt:
"Blood Debt" Write a vivid third-person narrative depicting an unsanctioned underground duel between two talented but hot-headed martial artists. Their schools have been embroiled in a generations-old rivalry, and despite strict prohibitions from their masters, these two younger fighters arrange a secret confrontation fueled by personal pride and simmering animosity. - **One fighter** represents **Jigen-ryū**, a traditional, aggressive style practiced historically by elite samurai clans. Its practitioners emphasize explosive offense, decisive strikes, and overwhelming speed. Known historically for their arrogance and disdain toward outsiders, practitioners consider their art superior and untouchable. - **The opposing fighter** employs a form of historical **Piguaquan**, a Chinese martial art traditionally practiced by commoners, bodyguards, and street fighters. This style emphasizes whipping, circular strikes, rapid footwork, unpredictable angles of attack, and evasive maneuvers. Practitioners often come from lower social classes and view the traditional elite as pretentious and detached from true combat effectiveness. The fight takes place in the crumbling ruins of an abandoned temple, now functioning as a hidden arena for illicit duels. Passionate supporters from both factions surround them, wagering money, pride, and reputation on the outcome. **Essential Instructions:** Maintain absolute physical coherence throughout—clearly track fighters' positions relative to each other and their environment. Precisely depict strikes, counters, momentum shifts, and spatial dynamics, allowing readers to clearly visualize the mechanics and choreography of the fight at every moment. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
The air was heavy with tension as the two fighters, Kaito and Ryota, stood facing each other in the crumbling ruins of the abandoned temple. The once-majestic structure now lay in ruins, its walls cracked and worn, its stones overgrown with vines and moss. The only sound was the soft rustling of leaves and the distant murmur of the crowd, their voices hushed but their excitement palpable. Kaito, a skilled practitioner of Jigen-ryū, wore a red armband with a silver emblem of a samurai's crest. His eyes blazed with a fierce intensity, his jaw set in a determined line. Ryota, on the other hand, wore a black armband with a silver emblem of a dragon's head. His eyes gleamed with a quiet confidence, his movements economical and precise. The two fighters had been at odds for years, their schools locked in a bitter rivalry that spanned generations. Kaito's Jigen-ryū masters saw Ryota's Piguaquan as nothing more than a crude, unsophisticated art, fit only for commoners and street fighters. Ryota's followers, meanwhile, viewed Jigen-ryū as arrogant and pretentious, a relic of a bygone era that had lost touch with the true demands of combat. Despite the strict prohibitions from their masters, the two fighters had secretly arranged a duel, fueled by personal pride and simmering animosity. The crowd, a mixture of passionate supporters from both factions, had gathered to witness the clash of styles, their money, pride, and reputation riding on the outcome. As the two fighters circled each other, their eyes locked in a fierce stare. Kaito, known for his explosive offense and decisive strikes, cracked his knuckles in anticipation. Ryota, with his whip-like movements and unpredictable angles of attack, smiled to himself, his eyes glinting with amusement. The crowd erupted into cheers as the referee, a neutral third party, shouted the signal to begin. Kaito charged forward, his feet pounding the earth in a blur of speed. Ryota dodged and weaved, his body a fluid, circular motion as he sought to evade the Jigen-ryū master's attacks. Kaito's first strike was a lightning-fast slash of his katana, aimed squarely at Ryota's chest. But the Piguaquan practitioner was no slouch, and he sidestepped the attack with ease, his feet barely touching the ground. As Kaito recovered, Ryota launched a whirlwind of whipping punches and kicks, each strike aimed at Kaito's exposed flank. Kaito danced back, his movements a blur of speed and agility as he sought to evade the Piguaquan master's attacks. But Ryota was relentless, his body a coiled spring, ready to unleash a devastating combination at any moment. The two fighters clashed, their movements a mesmerizing dance of steel and fists, each strike and counterstrike a testament to their skill and training. The crowd roared, their voices a deafening din as the two fighters exchanged blows. Kaito's Jigen-ryū style was a force to be reckoned with, its explosive offense and decisive strikes capable of shattering steel. But Ryota's Piguaquan was a more subtle art, its emphasis on evasive maneuvers and unpredictable angles of attack making it a nightmare to defend against. As the fight wore on, the two fighters began to tire. Kaito's breathing grew ragged, his movements slowing as Ryota's relentless pressure began to take its toll. But the Piguaquan master was no stranger to combat, and he drew upon his years of experience to push Kaito to his limits. The two fighters clashed in a final, desperate bid for victory. Kaito's katana flashed in the fading light, its blade slicing through the air with deadly precision. Ryota dodged and weaved, his body a fluid, circular motion as he sought to evade the Jigen-ryū master's attacks. And then, in a flash of inspiration, Ryota saw his chance. With a swift, economical motion, he launched a devastating combination of kicks and punches, each strike aimed squarely at Kaito's vulnerable points. The Jigen-ryū master stumbled back, his movements slowing as Ryota's attacks began to take their toll. The crowd erupted into cheers as Ryota landed a final, crushing blow, sending Kaito crashing to the ground. The Piguaquan master stood over his defeated opponent, his chest heaving with exertion, his eyes gleaming with triumph. As the referee declared Ryota the winner, the crowd surged forward, their voices a deafening din as they congratulated the Piguaquan master on his victory. Kaito, meanwhile, struggled to his feet, his face set in a determined line. Despite his defeat, he knew that he had earned Ryota's respect, and that their rivalry was far from over. The two fighters shook hands, their movements a gesture of respect and admiration. As they parted ways, the crowd dispersed, their voices fading into the distance as the two fighters vanished into the night, their styles and traditions forever changed by the clash of steel and fists.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response is a competent but deeply generic piece of fight writing. It fulfills the prompt's broad strokes—two rival fighters, an abandoned temple, warring factions, a secret duel—but does almost nothing with the specific instructions that made the prompt interesting. The demand for "absolute physical coherence" and precise choreography is largely ignored: the fight is a series of vague abstractions ("a whirlwind of whipping punches," "a mesmerizing dance of steel and fists") with no tracked positions, no concrete spatial logic, and no moment a reader could actually diagram. The katana appears without setup—Kaito simply has one mid-fight—which is a notable physical incoherence. The temple setting is described once and then abandoned; the crowd is a faceless roar. The ending, with a handshake and mutual respect, is a stock beat that flattens the "simmering animosity" the prompt demanded, and the "styles and traditions forever changed" line is unearned. Prose is repetitive ("his body a fluid, circular motion" appears nearly verbatim twice), heavy on adjectives and telling ("his eyes blazed with a fierce intensity"), and the middle section reads like filler padding toward word count. There is no dialogue, no distinct character voice, and the two fighters are interchangeable apart from their armbands. The fight's momentum shifts are asserted rather than shown ("the two fighters began to tire"). It is coherent and readable, but bland, clichéd, and emotionally flat. + Physical-spatial: Breaking Formation — Score: 32.0
Prompt:
**"Breaking Formation"** Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of a street dancer from a rough neighborhood who's won a scholarship to an elite K-pop training academy in Seoul. Desperately out of place culturally and technically, they struggle with a militaristically precise choreography that contradicts everything about their improvisational background. The punishing rehearsal takes place under a famous instructor known for breaking students to remake them, who sees potential in the narrator's raw talent but not their attitude. This scene MUST maintain complete physical coherence—each dance move, position shift, and spatial relationship must be tracked with precision throughout the studio. Paint an exceptionally vivid kinetic picture through descriptions that capture not just movement but the contrast between the narrator's fluid, expressive style and the mechanical precision demanded. Their internal monologue should reveal culture shock, class anxiety, and the threatening realization that success here might mean losing their authentic movement voice. Include technical terminology that highlights the collision of street and commercial styles, moments when muscle memory betrays them, and a crucial decision point about whether to conform or rebel. Physical clarity is essential—readers must be able to visualize exactly how bodies move through space, how rhythms align or clash, and how the protagonist's body struggles against unfamiliar discipline. 1000 words.
Model Output:
I stepped into the studio, my eyes scanning the room for a glimpse of familiarity. Instead, I was met with rows of mirrored walls, a sea of eager faces, and the unmistakable scent of sweat and discipline. I'd won a scholarship to this elite K-pop training academy in Seoul, a dream I'd nurtured since I was a teenager, dancing on the streets of my rough neighborhood. But nothing could have prepared me for the culture shock that hit me like a ton of bricks. Instructor Lee, a woman notorious for breaking students to remake them, stood at the front of the room, her eyes piercing as she surveyed us. Her gaze landed on me, and I felt a shiver run down my spine. I'd heard stories about her unyielding approach, how she'd push students to their limits, and beyond. I wondered if I was ready to face that kind of scrutiny. The music began, a pulsating beat that seemed to reverberate through every cell in my body. The instructors, a trio of seasoned dancers, started to demonstrate the choreography, their movements a blur of precision and control. I watched, mesmerized, as they executed intricate footwork, their bodies weaving in and out of each other like a choreographed ballet. But when it was my turn to join in, my body rebelled. My feet stumbled over the intricate footwork, my arms flailing wildly as I tried to keep up. The instructors' eyes narrowed, their expressions a mixture of disappointment and disdain. I felt a pang of anxiety, my mind racing with the thought of failure. As I struggled to keep pace, I couldn't help but think of my street dancing roots. I'd grown up dancing on the streets, improvising to the rhythm of the city. My movements were fluid, expressive, and raw. But this...this was something else entirely. The choreography was militaristic, precise, and cold. It felt like a prison, a prison that I was desperate to escape. I glanced around the room, searching for a lifeline. That's when I saw him – a tall, lanky dancer with a messy mop of hair and a look of quiet intensity. He was watching me, his eyes filled with a mixture of curiosity and concern. I felt a spark of connection, a sense that maybe, just maybe, I wasn't alone in this. The music shifted, the beat growing faster and more complex. I felt my body start to respond, my feet tapping out the rhythm in a way that felt almost...natural. But when I looked down, I saw that my arms were still flailing, my movements a jarring contrast to the precision of the other dancers. Instructor Lee's voice cut through the music, her words a stern reminder of the importance of discipline. "Focus, focus, focus!" she bellowed. "You can't just dance, you have to follow the choreography!" I felt a surge of frustration, my mind rebelling against the strictures of the academy. Why couldn't I just be myself? Why did I need to conform to this rigid, mechanical style? But as I looked around the room, I saw the other dancers, their faces set in determined lines. They were all in this together, and I knew I couldn't give up. I took a deep breath, my body relaxing into the music. I let my arms drop, my feet slowing to a crawl. And then, something strange happened. My body started to respond, my movements becoming more fluid, more expressive. I felt a sense of freedom, a sense of release. But it was short-lived. As I caught up to the other dancers, I realized that I'd fallen behind. The music had changed, the beat growing faster and more complex. I felt my heart racing, my breath coming in short gasps. Instructor Lee's voice cut through the music once more, her words a harsh reminder of my failure. "You're not listening, you're not following. You're a liability, a distraction." I felt a surge of anger, my mind rebelling against the instructor's words. But as I looked around the room, I saw the other dancers, their faces set in determined lines. They were all in this together, and I knew I couldn't give up. I took a deep breath, my body relaxing into the music. I let my arms drop, my feet slowing to a crawl. And then, something strange happened. My body started to respond, my movements becoming more fluid, more expressive. I felt a sense of freedom, a sense of release. But this time, it was different. This time, I was in control. I was dancing on my own terms, my movements a fusion of street and commercial styles. I felt a sense of pride, a sense of accomplishment. As the music faded, Instructor Lee's eyes narrowed, her expression a mixture of surprise and curiosity. "You're a wild card," she said, her voice low and husky. "You've got potential, but you need to learn to control it." I felt a surge of excitement, my mind racing with the possibilities. I knew that I still had a long way to go, but for the first time since I arrived at the academy, I felt like I belonged. I felt like I was home. As I left the studio, I felt a sense of determination, a sense of purpose. I knew that I had a long road ahead of me, but I was ready. I was ready to take on the challenges of the academy, to push myself to new heights. And I was ready to lose myself in the music, to let my body speak for itself. The music still echoed in my ears as I walked out of the studio, the beat pulsating through my veins like lifeblood. I knew that I would never be the same again, that I had been forever changed by this experience. And I knew that I would always carry the academy with me, a part of me that I would never be able to shake. As I walked through the streets of Seoul, I felt a sense of wonder, a sense of awe. The city was alive, the music pulsating through every cell in my body. I felt like I was a part of it, like I was a thread in the intricate tapestry of the city's rhythm. And I knew that I would never be alone again, that I had found my tribe, my family. We were a tribe of misfits, of outcasts, of dancers who refused to be bound by the rules. We were a tribe of rebels, of renegades, of dancers who dared to be different. As I disappeared into the crowd, I felt a sense of pride, a sense of belonging. I was home, and I knew that I would never be alone again.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece opens with a serviceable premise but quickly reveals significant weaknesses. The prose is heavily reliant on cliché ("hit me like a ton of bricks," "piercing eyes," "a shiver ran down my spine") and on repetitive sentence constructions ("I felt a surge of...", "I felt a sense of..."). The emotional arc is told rather than shown: the narrator repeatedly announces anxiety, frustration, and belonging rather than dramatizing them through concrete physical detail. The prompt's central demands are largely unmet. There is no technical dance terminology—no naming of moves, counts, styles, or the street/commercial collision in specific terms. The "militaristic precision" is asserted abstractly ("intricate footwork," "precision and control") without any actual choreographic specificity. Physical coherence is weak: the narrator's body is described in vague, contradictory terms ("arms flailing," then "movements becoming more fluid"), and the studio's spatial layout is never established. The crucial decision point—conform or rebel—is fudged: the narrator simply "relaxes into the music" and magically achieves a "fusion of street and commercial styles" without any concrete moment of choice or technique. Structurally, the piece is repetitive to the point of incoherence. Two nearly identical passages appear verbatim ("I took a deep breath, my body relaxing into the music... a sense of freedom, a sense of release"), suggesting the model lost track of its own narrative. The lanky dancer introduced as a potential connection is dropped entirely. Instructor Lee's shift from calling the narrator "a liability" to "a wild card" with "potential" happens in a single beat with no intervening development—an unearned transformation. The ending drifts into vague uplift ("I found my tribe, my family... I was home") that contradicts the established conflict about losing one's authentic movement voice, which is never meaningfully explored. Dialogue is minimal and generic ("Focus, focus, focus!"). The tone is consistent but consistently flat and amateurish. The piece is coherent in the loosest sense—events follow in order—but it meanders through repeated emotional beats and ends on an incongruously positive note that the narrative has not earned. + Gothic-fantasy-humour: Court of the Dreaming — Score: 44.8
Prompt:
Court of the Dreaming: Territorial Dream Dispute. Write a dark fantasy scene with comedic elements set in the Courts of Dream where a case is being heard to determine ownership of a contested dreamscape. Mora, the Slavic nightmare spirit who sits on sleepers' chests causing paralysis and terror, has recently invaded and subverted a carefully crafted recurring dream of a schoolboy, Max. The dream originally featured the boy lying in a field of wildflowers with his crush, always culminating in a muddled almost-kiss and a pleasant confusion of emotions. This dream was the meticulous work of "First Love" (a rosy-cheeked, slightly bashful entity who crafts formative romantic experiences). However, Mora has recently infiltrated the dreamscape, transforming the scene so that when the girl playfully mounts the boy, she morphs into a terrifying yet somehow still confusingly attractive vision who proceeds to sit on his chest and feed on his breath. Mora's defense largely consists of the practical argument that "a girl's gotta eat too, you know?" Write this scene as it unfolds in the courtroom of the Dreaming, with Dream's demeanour, typically impenetrable, betraying a sense of irritation at having to adjudicate such matters. The comedy should derive from the farcical situation, familiar elements of courtroom drama intersecting with the fantastical world of the Dreaming, and the droll portrayal of the characters and their interactions. The humor should be dark and understated throughout. Dream is tempted to some mischief in the resolution of the case. Use descriptives sparingly when setting scene. Write for readability & engagement. 1000 words.
Model Output:
The courtroom of the Dreaming was a dimly lit, labyrinthine space where the surreal and the bizarre dwelled. Dream, the omnipotent arbiter of the subconscious, sat behind a worn, velvet-draped bench. Her expression, a mask of serene detachment, betrayed a hint of exasperation as she listened to the litigants. Before her stood First Love, a rosy-cheeked entity with a bashful demeanor, and Mora, the Slavic nightmare spirit with an unsettling aura of hunger. The air was heavy with the weight of conflicting emotions as they awaited the verdict. "State your case," Dream rapped her bony finger on the armrest, her voice like the soft lapping of waves on a still pond. First Love fidgeted, adjusting her gown, a delicate confection of petals and moonbeams. "Your Honor, esteemed members of the Court, I bring forth a matter of utmost importance. A dream, crafted with care and devotion, has been subverted. My dream, a tender scene of first love, has been desecrated by...by...this creature." Mora snorted, a low, rumbling sound that sent shivers down the spines of the gathered onlookers. "Hey, I was just trying to make a living. A girl's gotta eat, you know? I figured, why not try my hand at the whole 'creepy, unsettling love interest' thing?" Dream's eyes narrowed, her gaze piercing the mist that clung to her face like a damp shroud. "You claim my right to dream, to create and shape the subconscious, has been infringed upon. How do you justify your actions, Mora?" Mora shrugged, her long, spindly arms waving in a gesture of nonchalance. "Hey, it's just a living. I needed something to fill the void. And this kid was just so...lively. I figured, why not take advantage of the opportunity?" The courtroom erupted into a cacophony of whispers and gasps. First Love's face turned a deep shade of crimson as she clutched her chest. "How dare you! This was a work of art, a masterpiece of the subconscious! You have no right to desecrate it!" Dream raised a hand, and the murmurs ceased. "Enough. Mora, you have presented your defense. First Love, you have also presented your claim. Now, I shall render my verdict." The hourglass, a symbol of the Dreaming's own precarious balance, seemed to tremble in its stands, the sand trickling ominously as Dream deliberated. As she pondered, the courtroom fell silent, the air thick with anticipation. First Love shifted uncomfortably, her eyes darting about the room, while Mora leaned back in her seat, a wicked glint in her eye. Dream's expression remained impassive, yet a hint of mischief danced in her gaze. She tapped her chin thoughtfully, the sound echoing through the chamber like a rusty gate. "Hmm...I must consider the nature of the dream, the intent behind its creation, and the rights of the subconscious. Mora, do you understand the concept of ownership in the realm of dreams?" Mora snorted, her voice dripping with sarcasm. "Oh, yeah, I'm an expert on that. I mean, who doesn't love a good fight over who gets to claim the spotlight in someone's subconscious?" First Love's eyes widened, her face pale. "That's not the point, Mora! This was a dream, a creation of my own making. You have no right to claim it as your own!" Dream's gaze turned to First Love, her eyes glinting with a hint of amusement. "Ah, but that is exactly the problem. A dream is not owned in the classical sense. It is a fleeting, ephemeral thing, a manifestation of the subconscious. And yet, Mora, you have taken it upon yourself to claim it as your own...if only for the purpose of feeding on its emotions." The courtroom erupted into a chorus of gasps and whispers, with some onlookers nodding in agreement and others jeering at Mora's audacity. Dream's expression turned thoughtful, her gaze drifting back to the sand at the bottom of the hourglass. "I propose a compromise. Mora, you shall take on a... residuum of the dream. A small portion, a fraction of its essence. This will satiate your hunger, and First Love, you shall retain the bulk of your creation." Mora's face lit up, a wicked grin spreading across her face. "Sounds like a deal to me!" First Love's face contorted in distress, her hands fluttering about like a bird in a gilded cage. "But, Your Honor, this is madness! I won't let you take any of my creation away!" Dream's expression turned cold, her voice dripping with a hint of sweetness. "Silence, First Love. You have been warned. The compromise is binding. And, Mora, do try to keep the, ah... 'enthusiasm' to a minimum. We wouldn't want any...unpleasantness in the courtroom." As the verdict was read, the sand in the hourglass continued its slow trickle, a reminder of the delicate balance of the Dreaming. Mora's grin faltered, her eyes flicking towards First Love, a hint of unease creeping into her demeanor. The courtroom erupted into a flurry of whispers and murmurs, the onlookers exchanging nervous glances. First Love's face twisted in a mixture of relief and despair, while Mora's eyes gleamed with a hungry light, her gaze lingering on the defeated dreamer. Dream's gaze turned to Mora, a hint of mischief dancing in her eyes. "And, Mora? Do be sure to...remember your duties. I wouldn't want any...mishaps in the future." Mora's smile grew wider, her voice dripping with malice. "Oh, I'll remember. I'll make sure to savor every morsel of that dream. Every morsel." As the court adjourned, the courtroom emptied, with the onlookers whispering among themselves, the sound of Mora's laughter echoing through the empty halls, a faint, unsettling whisper that lingered in the shadows.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response establishes a courtroom in the Dreaming and stages the dispute between First Love and Mora, but it does so with heavy reliance on stock phrasing and repetitive beats. The prompt asks for dark comedy, understated humor, courtroom farce, and Dream's irritation and temptation to mischief; the response gestures at all of these but executes them thinly. Strengths: the basic premise is preserved—Mora's "a girl's gotta eat" defense appears, First Love's bashfulness is present, and Dream delivers a compromise verdict with a hint of mischief. The scene is readable and coherent on a plot level. Weaknesses: The humor is almost entirely absent in practice. Mora's dialogue is flippant in a generic, sitcom-ish way ("Hey, it's just a living") rather than darkly droll, and the same jokes and gestures repeat ("a girl's gotta eat," snorting, shrugging, "wicked grin"). The courtroom farce the prompt requested—procedural absurdity, objections, evidence, cross-examination—is barely explored; the trial consists of vague statements of claim and an immediate verdict. Dream is rendered as female without explanation, which may jar readers expecting the Sandman character, and her "impenetrable demeanor betraying irritation" is stated rather than dramatized. The prose leans on clichés: "voice like the soft lapping of waves," "mask of serene detachment," "wicked glint in her eye," "hands fluttering about like a bird in a gilded cage." Emotions are repeatedly told rather than shown ("The air was heavy with the weight of conflicting emotions"). The ending is muddled—the verdict's mechanics ("a residuum of the dream") are vague, First Love's reaction is contradictory, and the closing image of Mora's laughter is atmospheric but unearned, since the mischief Dream was tempted to is never concretely realized. The piece also underuses its richest material: the boy Max, the almost-kiss, the confusingly attractive nightmare—none of the specific, comic-dark texture of the prompt's scenario makes it into the scene. The hourglass is introduced as a symbol and then merely re-mentioned. Overall, a serviceable but flat, repetitive draft that misses the tonal precision and comic timing the prompt demanded. + Fanfic-crossover: Blood Moon — Score: 40.5
Prompt:
Blood Moon Alucard & Yennefer Write a story where Alucard (Hellsing) encounters Yennefer (The Witcher) in medieval Europe. Yennefer's magical abilities intrigue the vampire, while she's both repulsed and fascinated by his monstrous nature. Their power dynamic shifts constantly as they circle each other, neither willing to show weakness first. **Alucard (Hellsing)** Alucard is an ancient, immensely powerful vampire who works for the Hellsing Organization. He can regenerate from almost any wound and shape-shift at will. He's sadistic, arrogant, and takes pleasure in toying with his enemies before killing them. Despite being a monster, he despises weak vampires who can't accept their nature. He wears a distinctive red coat, wide-brimmed hat, and orange sunglasses. He uses massive handguns loaded with silver bullets. He seeks worthy opponents who can challenge him and potentially end his immortal existence. Famous quote: "The bird of Hermes is my name, eating my wings to make me tame." **Yennefer of Vengerberg (The Witcher)** Yennefer is a powerful sorceress who magically altered her appearance to hide her congenital hunchback. She's ambitious, stubborn, and manipulative when necessary. She has violet eyes and smells of lilac and gooseberries. Despite her cold exterior, she desperately wants to reverse her infertility caused by magical transformation. She's centuries old but appears young. She's politically savvy and refuses to be underestimated. She has a sarcastic sense of humor and little patience for fools. Though she rarely shows it, she's capable of deep love and loyalty. Famous quote: "I'm not sweet and I'm not a maiden in distress in need of saving." Third person, present tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
The moon hung low in the sky, casting an eerie glow over the medieval village. Alucard, dressed in his signature red coat and wide-brimmed hat, strode through the village, his eyes scanning the surroundings with a mixture of curiosity and disdain. He had been tracking a lead on a potential opponent, a vampire with a reputation for being nearly unbeatable in battle. As he walked, the crunch of gravel beneath his boots echoed through the stillness. He spotted a figure standing atop a nearby hill, gazing out at the village. The figure's features were shrouded in shadows, but Alucard's exceptional eyesight picked up on the telltale signs of magic emanating from her. "Ah, a witch," Alucard said, his voice low and smooth. "How... quaint." The figure turned, and Alucard's gaze landed on Yennefer of Vengerberg. Her violet eyes sparkled in the moonlight, and her gaze narrowed as she took in Alucard's imposing figure. "You're a long way from home, aren't you?" she said, her voice husky and confident. Alucard chuckled, his eyes crinkling at the corners. "I'm a vampire, witch. I can appear anywhere, anytime. You're just a relic of a bygone era, clinging to your little tricks and potions." Yennefer's eyes flashed with amusement. "And you're a relic of a bygone era, playing dress-up in your little red coat and silly hat." Alucard's smile grew wider, his fangs glinting in the moonlight. "Ah, but this coat is not silly. It's a symbol of my superiority. And my hat... well, it's just a little something to add to my charm." Yennefer's gaze lingered on the hat, her expression unreadable. "I'm sure it's... fascinating. But tell me, vampire, what brings you to this village?" "I'm looking for someone," Alucard said, his eyes locked on hers. "A vampire with a certain... reputation. But I think you might know something about him." Yennefer raised an eyebrow, her curiosity piqued. "Oh? And what makes you think I'd know anything about that particular individual?" Alucard shrugged, his shoulders barely moving beneath his coat. "You have a certain... reputation, witch. One that precedes you. I'm sure you'll admit to knowing nothing, of course." Yennefer's smile was a slow, deliberate thing, like a snake uncoiling its body. "I know a great many things, Alucard. And I'm sure you'll find that our conversation is... enlightening." The two vampires stood there, locked in a silent game of cat and mouse. Alucard's eyes never left hers, his gaze burning with intensity. Yennefer's eyes, on the other hand, seemed to bore into his very soul, as if searching for weaknesses. As the tension between them grew, the air seemed to vibrate with anticipation. Alucard's hand resting on the grip of his gun, his fingers drumming a slow rhythm on the handle. Yennefer's eyes never leaving his face, her hands clasped behind her back, her fingers drumming a matching beat on the folds of her cloak. The silence between them grew thicker, more oppressive, until it seemed to be a living, breathing thing. Alucard's gaze never wavered, his eyes never blinking as he watched Yennefer. And Yennefer's gaze never faltered, her eyes locked on his with an unnerving intensity. It was as if they were two predators circling each other, each waiting for the other to make the first move. And in that moment, it was clear that neither would be the first to back down. The air seemed to hold its breath, waiting for the inevitable confrontation. But Alucard and Yennefer just stood there, locked in a silent standoff, each waiting for the other to make the first move. And in that moment, it seemed that the outcome was far from certain.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece establishes a moonlit medieval setting and brings Alucard and Yennefer into contact, but it fails to deliver on nearly every element of the prompt. The most glaring error: the text refers to "the two vampires" — Yennefer is not a vampire, a basic character error that undermines believability. The prompt also specifies third person, present tense; the piece is written in past tense, a direct instruction violation. The requested ~1000 words is also not met (roughly 700). Characterization is thin. Alucard's dialogue is generic and lacks his sadistic menace or distinctive voice; his famous arrogance comes across as mild banter about his hat. Yennefer fares slightly better — her sarcasm ("playing dress-up in your little red coat and silly hat") is in the right register — but she's given little agency and no display of her actual magical power, despite the prompt specifying her abilities intrigue Alucard. The "repulsed and fascinated" dynamic is absent; the piece settles into a flat standoff. The power dynamic never shifts — it's static from beginning to end. Structurally, the story is severely underplotted. The middle section is repetitive filler: multiple paragraphs restate the same circling-predators standoff with slightly varied wording ("two predators circling each other," "silent game of cat and mouse," "silent standoff"). Nothing happens. No confrontation, no revelation, no resolution — the piece simply stops, ending on the non-ending "the outcome was far from certain," which reads as an admission the author didn't know where to go. The opening hook (Alucard tracking a vampire with a reputation) is introduced and then abandoned entirely. Prose quality is mediocre: clichés ("the air seemed to hold its breath," "cat and mouse," "bore into his very soul"), awkward constructions ("Alucard's hand resting on the grip of his gun, his fingers drumming" — a sentence fragment), and grammatical slips ("The two vampires stood there"). Dialogue is serviceable but bland, with none of the wit or menace either character is known for. Emotionally, the piece is inert — tension is asserted rather than built. On the positive side: it is coherent, easy to follow, and doesn't descend into purple prose or overwrought melodrama. The pacing problems are of stagnation rather than chaos. But as a piece fulfilling this specific prompt, it's a failure of imagination and instruction-following. + Fanfic-crossover: Prison Strings — Score: 47.8
Prompt:
Prison Strings Jolyne Cujoh & Makima Write a story where Jolyne Cujoh (JoJo's Bizarre Adventure) and Makima (Chainsaw Man) cross paths at a supernatural prison facility. Jolyne is immediately suspicious of Makima's falsely sweet demeanor, while Makima is intrigued by Jolyne's Stand ability. Their battle of wits escalates dangerously as Jolyne realizes how manipulative Makima truly is. **Jolyne Cujoh (JoJo's Bizarre Adventure)** Jolyne is the daughter of Jotaro Kujo and the first female JoJo protagonist. Her Stand, Stone Free, allows her to unravel her body into string that she can control. She's hot-headed, foul-mouthed, and doesn't take disrespect from anyone. She was framed for murder and sent to prison. She has a distinctive butterfly tattoo on her arm and wears her hair in two buns. Despite her tough exterior, she's deeply loyal to her friends and family. She can be impulsive but is highly adaptable in battle. She inherited her father's ability to stay cool under pressure but is more emotionally expressive. Famous quote: "If there's no Mickey, this shit ain't Disney!" **Makima (Chainsaw Man)** Makima appears as a young woman with auburn hair and ringed yellow eyes. She's the Control Devil who can dominate anyone she considers beneath her (nearly everyone). She maintains a pleasant, soft-spoken demeanor that masks her manipulative, cold nature. She can create contracts with humans and devils, often exploiting their desires. She's obsessed with Chainsaw Man and wants to control him. She keeps numerous people and devils as pets on leashes. She's practically immortal due to a contract with the Prime Minister. She views humans as lesser beings but mimics human emotions perfectly. Famous quote: "It's okay. Dogs are supposed to die for their owners." Third person, present tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
The sun beat down on the supernatural prison facility, casting a golden glow over the chain-link fences that surrounded the yard. Jolyne Cujoh, her hair tied in two buns, her butterfly tattoo on full display, walked along the fence, her eyes scanning the area for any signs of trouble. Her Stand, Stone Free, hummed quietly, ready to spring into action at a moment's notice. As she turned a corner, she spotted a woman with auburn hair and ringed yellow eyes, chatting with a group of guards. Makima, the infamous Control Devil, smiled sweetly as she listened to their conversation. Jolyne's instincts immediately went on high alert. There was something off about Makima's demeanor, something that didn't quite add up. Makima caught Jolyne's gaze and raised an eyebrow, a hint of amusement dancing in her eyes. "Ah, a new inmate, I see," Makima said, her voice dripping with sugary sweetness. "Welcome to our little community, dear. I'm Makima, the... caretaker of this lovely establishment." Jolyne's grip on her hands tightened, her fingers itching to wrap around Makima's throat. There was something manipulative about the woman, something that reeked of poison. "Just here for a visit," Jolyne replied, her tone even, but her eyes never leaving Makima's face. Makima chuckled, her eyes glinting with interest. "Oh, I'm sure you'll be staying a while. We have... arrangements in place to ensure your... comfort." Makima's smile grew wider, her eyes sparkling with amusement. Jolyne's Stand, Stone Free, stirred, sensing her tension. The air around Jolyne began to ripple, as if the very fabric of reality was responding to her emotions. Makima's gaze flickered, her eyes narrowing slightly, but she didn't flinch. "I'm sure you'll find our... accommodations satisfactory," Makima said, her voice dripping with condescension. "After all, we have the best... facilities in the business." Jolyne's anger simmered just below the surface. She knew that Makima was hiding something, that there was more to the woman than met the eye. She decided to test Makima's patience, to see how far she would push before the woman's facade cracked. "I'm not looking for 'comfort'," Jolyne spat, her voice low and menacing. "I'm looking for answers. What's going on in this place? What kind of 'arrangements' you talking about?" Makima's smile never wavered, but her eyes flashed with a warning. "Oh, dear, you're so... eager. I'm afraid you're not quite ready for the truth. But don't worry, I'll... enlighten you when the time is right." Jolyne's eyes narrowed, her mind racing with possibilities. She knew that Makima was a master manipulator, that she would stop at nothing to get what she wanted. The woman's words were laced with venom, with a subtle threat that sent a shiver down Jolyne's spine. "I'm not afraid of you," Jolyne lied, her voice steady, but her heart pounding in her chest. Makima's smile grew wider, her eyes glinting with amusement. "Oh, I'm shaking in my boots," she said, her voice dripping with sarcasm. "But don't worry, dear, I'll... take care of you. You'll see." The air around Jolyne seemed to thicken, as if the very atmosphere was responding to Makima's words. Jolyne's Stand, Stone Free, stirred, its energy building to a crescendo. She knew that she was walking a thin line, that one wrong move could spark a battle that would end in disaster. But Jolyne was not one to back down from a fight. She was a Joestar, after all, and Joestars were known for their bravery and their willingness to take risks. She smiled, a cold, hard smile, and said, "Bring it on, Makima. I'm ready for you." Makima's eyes flashed with surprise, but her smile never wavered. "Oh, dear, you're so... enthusiastic. I'm looking forward to... getting to know you better." The air around Jolyne seemed to ripple, as if the very fabric of reality was responding to her emotions. Makima's eyes locked onto hers, a challenge burning in their depths. The battle of wits had begun, and Jolyne was ready to play.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response is a serviceable but deeply flawed piece of fanfiction. The most immediate instruction failure is tense: the prompt explicitly requested third person, present tense, and the model wrote in past tense throughout. Length also falls short of the 1000-word seed (~800 words). The prompt asked for a battle of wits that "escalates dangerously" and for Jolyne to realize how manipulative Makima truly is; the piece instead delivers a static, repetitive verbal sparring match that never escalates beyond veiled threats, and ends with the conflict barely begun — no realization, no payoff, no resolution. Character work is thin. Jolyne's voice is generic tough-girl; her famous foul mouth and impulsiveness are muted into stock menace ("Bring it on"). Makima's menace is conveyed almost entirely through ellipses and adjectives ("dripping with sugary sweetness," "dripping with condescension," "dripping with sarcasm") — the same crutch repeated three times. Neither character does anything surprising or specific to their canon; the dialogue could be swapped between any "tough protagonist" and any "sweet villain" without loss. Stylistically, the prose is repetitive and tell-heavy: "the air around Jolyne seemed to ripple/thicken" appears multiple times as a vague gesture at tension; emotions are named ("Jolyne's anger simmered," "her heart pounding") rather than dramatized. The ellipsis-laden Makima dialogue becomes a tic. Stone Free never does anything — the Stand "stirs" twice and that's it, wasting the central hook of the crossover. The ending is a non-ending: "The battle of wits had begun" restates the premise rather than concluding anything. On the positive side, the piece is coherent, grammatically clean, and keeps both characters broadly in personality; the opening establishes setting efficiently. But it reads as an outline of a confrontation rather than a story, with no escalation, no discovery, and no stakes. + Scp: Notes from the Future — Score: 41.6
Prompt:
Write an SCP report about a researcher who begins finding handwritten notes from their future self, warning about a containment breach that hasn't happened yet. The notes become increasingly desperate and incoherent, sometimes containing information that contradicts known reality. Include the Foundation's attempts to determine if this is an actual temporal anomaly or psychological manipulation by a contained entity, and the researcher's struggle to trust their own perception. First person (researcher's perspective with interspersed notes), past tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
**SCP-XXXX: "The Prophetic Handwriting"** **Object Class:** Euclid **Special Containment Procedures:** SCP-XXXX is to be housed in a standard anomalous item locker at Site-17. Access to SCP-XXXX is restricted to Level 3 personnel and above, with prior approval from at least two Level 4 personnel. Researchers are not allowed to keep the notes for themselves, and all copies must be stored in a secure location. **Description:** SCP-XXXX is a set of handwritten notes, dated from approximately 02/15/20 to 03/01/20, written by a researcher (hereafter referred to as "Subject-XXXX") who was working on SCP-XXXX-1 at Site-17. The notes began appearing in the researcher's workspace on 02/15/20, and continued until 03/01/20, when the researcher suddenly ceased writing. The notes appear to be written by Subject-XXXX's future self, who claims to have knowledge of an impending containment breach that has not yet occurred. The notes become increasingly desperate and incoherent as they progress, sometimes containing information that contradicts known reality. The handwriting is consistent in style and quality throughout the notes, but ink analysis reveals that the ink used does not match any standard office pen. **Initial Notes (02/15/20)** Dear Future Self, I'm starting to feel like something's off. I've been noticing strange occurrences around the lab, like equipment malfunctioning and strange noises at night. I've tried to brush it off as fatigue, but the feeling persists. - [Initial Entry] **Later Notes (02/18/20)** Dear Future Self, I had a dream last night that I was trapped in a never-ending loop of SCP-XXXX-1's containment procedures. I was reliving the same events over and over, and I couldn't escape. I woke up feeling anxious and disoriented. I've started to wonder if I'm losing my mind. - [Entry 2] **Desperate Notes (02/25/20)** Dear Future Self, I don't know what's happening to me. I've been seeing things out of the corner of my eye - shadowy figures, whispers in the darkness. I've tried to talk to the other researchers, but they just think I'm paranoid. I'm starting to think that I'm not who I think I am. - [Entry 5] **Incoherent Notes (03/01/20)** [Scrawled in a frantic, illegible hand] The bubble is growing. It's going to burst. We're all going to die. [Tracing of a crude diagram of a containment cell] The Foundation is not what it seems. [Scattered, cryptic phrases] [End of notes] **Incident Report** On 03/02/20, the researcher (Subject-XXXX) was taken into custody for a psychological evaluation. During the evaluation, it was discovered that Subject-XXXX had been experiencing vivid hallucinations and paranoia, which were later attributed to a combination of fatigue, stress, and a possible temporal anomaly. However, further analysis of the notes revealed that they contained information that contradicted known reality. For example, the note from 02/18/20 mentions a containment breach that had not yet occurred, and the note from 02/25/20 describes a shadowy figure that was not present in the lab at the time. The Foundation has been unable to determine whether SCP-XXXX is an actual temporal anomaly or if it is a result of psychological manipulation by a contained entity. The researcher's struggle to trust their own perception has made it difficult to discern fact from fiction. **Addendum** Following the incident, the Foundation has conducted a thorough review of the research and containment procedures. It has been determined that SCP-XXXX is not a temporal anomaly, but rather a result of the researcher's own psychological instability. The notes were likely the result of a combination of fatigue, stress, and a possible sleep disorder. However, the Foundation has also discovered that the notes contain fragments of information that suggest a possible connection to a real event. Further investigation is needed to determine the significance of these fragments and to understand the full extent of SCP-XXXX's anomalous properties. **Incident Report Update** On 03/15/20, a containment breach occurred at Site-17, resulting in the escape of SCP-XXXX-1. The breach was not predicted by any of the researchers or the Foundation, and it is unclear how SCP-XXXX-1 was able to escape. The incident has raised questions about the validity of SCP-XXXX and the reliability of the researcher's perception. Further investigation is needed to determine the cause of the breach and to understand the full extent of SCP-XXXX's anomalous properties. **Recommendations** In light of the recent containment breach, it is recommended that SCP-XXXX be reclassified as a Keter-level anomaly. Further research is needed to understand the full extent of SCP-XXXX's properties and to develop effective containment procedures. It is also recommended that the researcher who wrote the notes (Subject-XXXX) be given a thorough psychological evaluation and provided with counseling to address their mental health issues. **Addendum** Following the incident, the Foundation has discovered that the notes from SCP-XXXX contain a hidden message that reads: "Trust no one. The truth is not what it seems."
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response adopts a standard SCP document format but largely fails the prompt's core instructions. The prompt asked for first person, past tense, from the researcher's perspective with interspersed notes; the response instead delivers a detached third-person clinical report, with the "notes" reduced to short, generic diary entries addressed "Dear Future Self" — which inverts the premise (notes FROM the future self, not TO it). The escalating desperation and incoherence is asserted rather than rendered; the "incoherent" note is brief and tame. The Foundation's investigation into temporal anomaly vs. psychological manipulation is mentioned but shallow and contradictory — the Addendum declares it "not a temporal anomaly" due to stress, then a real breach occurs, then a hidden message appears, producing muddled causality rather than deliberate ambiguity. The ending's twist (the breach happens) is the obvious move and is delivered flatly. Prose is functional but bland, with repetitive phrasing ("Further investigation is needed" appears three times), internal inconsistencies (notes dated 02/18 "mention a containment breach" though the quoted note doesn't), and no distinct voice. The researcher's struggle to trust their own perception is told, not shown. Emotionally flat, mechanically formatted, and reads like a template filled in rather than a story. + Scp: Unmarked Doors Archive — Score: 34.8
Prompt:
Write an SCP entry about a series of identical unmarked doors appearing in abandoned buildings worldwide that lead to a massive, architecturally impossible archive. Documents within reference a previous global civilization destroyed by an unspecified containment failure. Foundation researchers discover references to their own organization under a different name, including personnel files for current staff dated centuries ago. Include exploration logs revealing sections of the archive actively being removed by entities resembling hazmat teams who flee when approached, and evidence that O5 Council members have visited the archive without authorization. Third person (exploration team perspective) with recovered document excerpts. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
**SCP-XXXX: "The Archive of the Unseen"** **Object Class:** Euclid **Special Containment Procedures:** All anomalous doors discovered within SCP-XXXX should be sealed and labeled with a standard "Do Not Enter" sign. The location of SCP-XXXX is to be kept secret from all personnel, and access to the archive is strictly prohibited without Level 3 clearance and explicit permission from at least two O5 Council members. Foundation researchers are encouraged to submit reports and findings on SCP-XXXX, but all documentation must be encrypted and stored on a secure server. **Description:** SCP-XXXX consists of a series of identical, unmarked doors that appear to have materialized in abandoned buildings worldwide. The doors are identical in size, shape, and material, but some exhibit varying degrees of weathering, suggesting they may be older than the surrounding structures. When opened, the doors lead to a massive, architecturally impossible archive containing vast amounts of information, documents, and artifacts. **Discovery:** On//20, a team of Foundation researchers discovered SCP-XXXX while investigating an abandoned building in New York City. The team, consisting of Dr., Dr., and Dr., entered the building through the anomalous door and discovered the archive. Initial exploration revealed a labyrinthine structure containing shelves upon shelves of documents, artifacts, and other items. As researchers delved deeper into the archive, they discovered references to a previous global civilization, which they later dubbed "The Old World." **Documentation:** Recovered documents within SCP-XXXX reference a catastrophic event known as "The Great Collapse," which led to the destruction of The Old World. The documents also mention a global organization known as "The Order of the New Dawn," which was responsible for containing and studying anomalous entities. Foundation researchers discovered personnel files for current staff members, dated centuries ago, which suggested that the organization had undergone significant changes over time. **Exploration Logs:** Following the initial discovery, a team of researchers, led by Dr., was dispatched to investigate SCP-XXXX further. The team encountered entities resembling hazmat teams, who were actively removing sections of the archive. When approached, these entities would flee, leaving behind evidence of their activities, including hazmat suits and tools. The team also discovered that O5 Council members had visited the archive without authorization. One entry, dated//20, reads: **O5 Council Log Entry 2020-02-15** "I've seen things in that archive that would make your blood run cold. It's like they're trying to erase themselves from existence. I've been seeing references to our own organization, but under a different name. It's like they're trying to cover their tracks. I don't know what's going on, but I know we need to be careful. This is bigger than we thought." **Addendum:** Following the discovery of O5 Council Log Entry 2020-02-15, the Foundation has increased security measures around SCP-XXXX, including the deployment of additional researchers and increased surveillance. **Incident Report:** On//20, a team of researchers, led by Dr., attempted to explore a section of the archive that had been removed by the hazmat teams. The team was ambushed by the hazmat teams, who were armed with unknown substances. The team was forced to retreat, and the hazmat teams escaped. **Recommendations:** Further research is required to understand the nature of SCP-XXXX and the entities responsible for removing sections of the archive. The Foundation should also investigate the implications of the O5 Council Log Entry 2020-02-15 and the potential connections between SCP-XXXX and the Old World. **Addendum 1:** Following the incident report, the Foundation has increased security measures around SCP-XXXX, including the deployment of additional researchers and increased surveillance. All personnel are reminded that access to the archive is strictly prohibited without Level 3 clearance and explicit permission from at least two O5 Council members. **Addendum 2:** A team of researchers, led by Dr., has been dispatched to explore a section of the archive that has been sealed off due to the hazmat teams. The team is expected to provide regular updates and findings.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] This SCP entry fulfills the prompt's bullet points only in the most superficial, checklist-like manner, and suffers from serious craft and formatting failures. Most damning is the presence of unfinished placeholder text throughout: "On//20," "Dr., Dr., and Dr.," "dated//20" — the model appears to have left template blanks unfilled, which is an unambiguous sign of an incomplete, unedited draft. Named characters are entirely absent, so character action, nuance, and believability cannot meaningfully be assessed (the personnel are all "Dr." placeholders). The prompt specified third-person exploration team perspective with recovered document excerpts. The response instead delivers a flat, summary-style document that recounts explorations rather than dramatizing them. The "Exploration Logs" section is a single paragraph of telling ("The team encountered entities resembling hazmat teams... they would flee") rather than an actual log with timestamps, dialogue, or sensory detail. The one "recovered document" — the O5 log entry — reads like a generic thriller line ("things that would make your blood run cold") and is not written in an institutional O5 voice; O5s do not write diary-style entries like this. The archive itself is never rendered. There is no imagery of the "architecturally impossible" space, no sense of scale, vertigo, or dread. The previous civilization, "The Great Collapse," and "The Order of the New Dawn" are named and dropped without texture. The hazmat entities are mentioned and flee off-page; the one "incident" is a one-line summary. The concept of Foundation personnel files dated centuries ago is stated but never explored for its existential horror — the single most interesting seed in the prompt is wasted. Structurally, the piece repeats itself (the same security-measures sentence appears twice verbatim in the Addenda), and the "Recommendations" section is bureaucratic filler. Object Class Euclid with doors that are supposed to be "sealed and labeled with a standard 'Do Not Enter' sign" is comically inadequate containment for a world-spanning anomaly, undermining the Foundation's established tone. Prose is serviceable at the sentence level but bland and report-clichéd. There is no dialogue, so Weak Dialogue is excluded. Tone is consistent (dully clinical), but consistently shallow. The piece is coherent only in the sense that it is grammatically parseable despite placeholders; its logic (hazmat teams "armed with unknown substances" who "ambushed" the team after previously fleeing) contradicts itself. On the positive side: the core ideas are present and the SCP format is roughly gestured at. But the execution is rushed, template-like, and amateurish, with no emotional or atmospheric payoff.
